Open Source Project Security Baseline | The Open Source Project Security (OSPS) Baseline is a set of security controls that projects should meet to demonstrate a strong security posture. The controls are organized by maturity level and category.              

Version: 2026.02.19

to top

*   [Overview](#overview)
*   [Controls Overview](#controls-overview)
*   [Access Control](#access-control)
*   [Build and Release](#build-and-release)
*   [Documentation](#documentation)
*   [Governance](#governance)
*   [Legal](#legal)
*   [Quality](#quality)

*   [Acknowledgments](#acknowledgments)

## Overview

The Open Source Project Security (OSPS) Baseline is a set of security criteria that projects should meet to demonstrate a strong security posture. The controls are organized by maturity level and category. In the detailed subsections you will find the control, rationale, and details notes.

Where possible, we have added control mappings to external frameworks. These are not guaranteed to be 100% matches, but instead serve as references to external elements that the Baseline maintainers believe relate to the Baseline control. This is not a functional connection, and does not imply that progress on one will necessarily result in progress on the other.

For more information on the project and to make contributions, visit the [GitHub repo](https://github.com/ossf/security-baseline).

* * *

## Controls Overview

*   [Level 1](#level-1): for any code or non-code project with any number of maintainers or users
*   [Level 2](#level-2): for any code project that has at least 2 maintainers and a small number of consistent users
*   [Level 3](#level-3): for any code project that has a large number of consistent users

### Level 1

**[OSPS-AC-01.01](#osps-ac-0101)**: When a [user](#user) attempts to read or modify a [sensitive resource](#sensitive-resource) in the [project](#project)'s authoritative [repository](#repository), the system MUST require the [user](#user) to complete a [multi-factor authentication](#multi-factor-authentication) process.

**[OSPS-AC-02.01](#osps-ac-0201)**: When a new [collaborator](#collaborator) is added, the [version control system](#version-control-system) MUST require manual permission assignment, or restrict the [collaborator](#collaborator) permissions to the lowest available privileges by default.

**[OSPS-AC-03.01](#osps-ac-0301)**: When a direct [commit](#commit) is attempted on the [project](#project)'s [primary branch](#primary-branch), an enforcement mechanism MUST prevent the [change](#change) from being applied.

**[OSPS-AC-03.02](#osps-ac-0302)**: When an attempt is made to delete the [project](#project)'s [primary branch](#primary-branch), the [version control system](#version-control-system) MUST treat this as a sensitive activity and require explicit confirmation of intent.

**[OSPS-BR-01.01](#osps-br-0101)**: Wh

66	        # which has no upstream fix (GO-2026-4887). testutil is test-infrastructure only and

68	        # continue-on-error: pgx/v5 CVEs have "Fixed in: N/A" upstream;
69	        # block will re-tighten once upstream patches land.
70	        continue-on-error: true

## OSPS-AC multi-factor authentication MUST

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Access Control > OSPS-AC-01 - Use [MFA](#multi-factor-authentication) for Sensitive Actions > OSPS-AC-01.01

#### OSPS-AC-01.01

**Requirement:** When a [user](#user) attempts to read or modify a [sensitive resource](#sensitive-resource) in the [project](#project)'s authoritative [repository](#repository), the system MUST require the [user](#user) to complete a [multi-factor authentication](#multi-factor-authentication) process.

**Recommendation:** Enforce multi-factor authentication for the project's version control system, requiring collaborators to provide a second form of authentication when accessing sensitive data or modifying repository settings. Passkeys are acceptable for this control.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1
*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Controls Overview > Level 1 (1)

### Level 1

**[OSPS-AC-01.01](#osps-ac-0101)**: When a [user](#user) attempts to read or modify a [sensitive resource](#sensitive-resource) in the [project](#project)'s authoritative [repository](#repository), the system MUST require the [user](#user) to complete a [multi-factor authentication](#multi-factor-authentication) process.

**[OSPS-AC-02.01](#osps-ac-0201)**: When a new [collaborator](#collaborator) is added, the [version control system](#version-control-system) MUST require manual permission assignment, or restrict the [collaborator](#collaborator) permissions to the lowest available privileges by default.

**[OSPS-AC-03.01](#osps-ac-0301)**: When a direct [commit](#commit) is attempted on the [project](#project)'s [primary branch](#primary-branch), an enforcement mechanism MUST prevent the [change](#change) from being applied.

**[OSPS-AC-03.02](#osps-ac-0302)**: When an attempt is made to delete the [project](#project)'s [primary branch](#primary-branch), the [version control system](#version-control-system) MUST treat this as a sensitive activity and require explicit confirmation of intent.

**[OSPS-BR-01.01](#osps-br-0101)**: When a [CI/CD pipeline](#ci-cd-pipeline) operates on untrusted metadata, those parameters MUST be sanitized and validated prior to use in the pipeline.

**[OSPS-BR-01.03](#osps-br-0103)**: When a [CI/CD pipeline](#ci-cd-pipeline) operates on untrusted [code](#code) snapshots, it MUST prevent access to privileged CI/CD credentials and asse

---

## OSPS-BR build release automated

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Build and Release > OSPS-BR-07 - Secure Secrets and Credentials > OSPS-BR-07.01

#### OSPS-BR-07.01

**Requirement:** The [project](#project) MUST prevent the unintentional storage of unencrypted [sensitive data](#sensitive-data), such as secrets and credentials, in the [version control system](#version-control-system).

**Recommendation:** Configure .gitignore or equivalent to exclude files that may contain sensitive information. Use pre-commit hooks and automated scanning tools to detect and prevent the inclusion of sensitive data in commits.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Controls Overview > Level 2 (1)

### Level 2

**[OSPS-AC-04.01](#osps-ac-0401)**: When a CI/CD task is executed with no permissions specified, the CI/CD system MUST default the task's permissions to the lowest permissions granted in the pipeline.

**[OSPS-BR-02.01](#osps-br-0201)**: When an official [release](#release) is created, that [release](#release) MUST be assigned a unique [version identifier](#version-identifier).

**[OSPS-BR-04.01](#osps-br-0401)**: When an official [release](#release) is created, that [release](#release) MUST contain a descriptive log of functional and security modifications.

**[OSPS-BR-05.01](#osps-br-0501)**: When a [build and release pipeline](#build-and-release-pipeline) ingests dependencies, it MUST use standardized tooling where available.

**[OSPS-BR-06.01](#osps-br-0601)**: When an official [release](#release) is created, that [release](#release) MUST be signed or accounted for in a signed manifest including each asset's cryptographic hashes.

**[OSPS-DO-06.01](#osps-do-0601)**: When the [project](#project) has made a [release](#release), the [project](#project) documentation MUST include a description of how the [project](#project) selects, obtains, and tracks its dependencies.

**[OSPS-DO-07.01](#osps-do-0701)**: The [project](#project) documentation MUST include instructions on how to build the software, including required libraries, frameworks, SDKs, and dependencies.

**[OSPS-GV-01.01](#osps-gv-0101)**: While active, the [project](#project) documentation MUST include…

---

## OSPS-BR cryptographic signing provenance SLSA

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Build and Release > OSPS-BR-06 - Include Signatures and Hashes With [Release](#release) > OSPS-BR-06.01

#### OSPS-BR-06.01

**Requirement:** When an official [release](#release) is created, that [release](#release) MUST be signed or accounted for in a signed manifest including each asset's cryptographic hashes.

**Recommendation:** Sign all released software assets at build time with a cryptographic signature or attestations, such as GPG or PGP signature, Sigstore signatures, SLSA provenance, or SLSA VSAs. Include the cryptographic hashes of each asset in a signed manifest or metadata file.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

---

## OSPS-BR SBOM

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Lexicon > Software Bill of Materials

### Software Bill of Materials

A list of all components that make up a given piece of software or hardware, formatted as CycloneDX or SPDX. This list must include the following data elements for the components included in the released software asset: license, supplier name, filename of the component, component name, component version, software identifiers, relationship between the components, author of the SBOM data and timestamp. Additionally, for deployable and executable components, the SBOM should record their cryptographic hashes.

**References:**

*   https://www.ntia.gov/sites/default/files/publications/sbom\_minimum\_elements\_report\_0.pdf
    
*   https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2023-04/sbom-types-document-508c.pdf
    
*   https://spdx.dev
    
*   https://cyclonedx.org

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Quality > OSPS-QA-02 - Publish Software Dependencies > OSPS-QA-02.02

#### OSPS-QA-02.02

**Requirement:** When the [project](#project) has made a [release](#release), all compiled released software assets MUST be delivered with a [software bill of materials](#software-bill-of-materials).

**Recommendation:** It is recommended to auto-generate SBOMs at build time using a tool that has been vetted for accuracy. This enables users to ingest this data in a standardized approach alongside other projects in their environment.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 3

---

## OSPS-DO README threat model

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Lexicon > Threat Modeling

### Threat Modeling

Threat modeling is an activity where the project looks at the codebase, associated processes and infrastructure, interfaces, key components and "thinks like a hacker" to brainstorm how the system be be broken or compromised.

Each identified threat is listed out so the project can then think about how to proactively avoid or close off any gaps/vulnerabilities that could arise.

Examples of threat modeling methodologies include the Shostack "4 Questions" model, STRIDE, and tools such as the Elevation of Privilege Threat Modeling Card Game or Threat Dragon.

**References:**

*   https://github.com/adamshostack/4QuestionFrame
    
*   https://owasp.org/www-community/Threat\_Modeling\_Process
    
*   https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/download/details.aspx?id=20303
    
*   https://owasp.org/www-project-threat-dragon

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Security Assessment > OSPS-SA-03 - Maintain a [Project](#project) Security Assessment > OSPS-SA-03.02

#### OSPS-SA-03.02

**Requirement:** When the [project](#project) has made a [release](#release), the [project](#project) MUST perform a threat modeling and [attack surface analysis](#attack-surface-analysis) to understand and protect against attacks on critical [code](#code) paths, functions, and interactions within the system.

**Recommendation:** Threat modeling is an activity where the project looks at the codebase, associated processes and infrastructure, interfaces, key components and "thinks like a hacker" and brainstorms how the system be be broken or compromised. Each identified threat is listed out so the project can then think about how to proactively avoid or close off any gaps/vulnerabilities that could arise. Ensure this is updated for new features or breaking changes.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 3

---

## OSPS-GV CONTRIBUTING code owners

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Governance > OSPS-GV-03 - Publish Contribution Guide > OSPS-GV-03.02

#### OSPS-GV-03.02

**Requirement:** While active, the [project](#project) documentation MUST include a guide for [code](#code) [contributors](#contributor) that includes requirements for acceptable contributions.

**Recommendation:** Extend the CONTRIBUTING.md or CONTRIBUTING/ contents in the project documentation to outline the requirements for acceptable contributions, including coding standards, testing requirements, and submission guidelines for code contributors. It is recommended that this guide is the source of truth for both contributors and approvers.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Governance > OSPS-GV-03 - Publish Contribution Guide > OSPS-GV-03.01

#### OSPS-GV-03.01

**Requirement:** While active, the [project](#project) documentation MUST include an explanation of the contribution process.

**Recommendation:** Create a CONTRIBUTING.md or CONTRIBUTING/ directory to outline the contribution process including the steps for submitting changes, and engaging with the project maintainers.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1
*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

---

## OSPS-LE OSI license SPDX

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Legal > OSPS-LE-02 - Ensure [Project](#project) [Licenses](#license) are Fully Open Source > OSPS-LE-02.01

#### OSPS-LE-02.01

**Requirement:** While active, the [license](#license) for the source [code](#code) MUST meet the OSI Open Source Definition or the FSF Free Software Definition.

**Recommendation:** Add a LICENSE file to the project's repo with a license that is an approved license by the Open Source Initiative (OSI), or a free license as approved by the Free Software Foundation (FSF). Examples of such licenses include the MIT, BSD 2-clause, BSD 3-clause revised, Apache 2.0, Lesser GNU General Public License (LGPL), and the GNU General Public License (GPL). Releasing to the public domain meets this control if there are no other encumbrances such as patents.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1
*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Legal > OSPS-LE-02 - Ensure [Project](#project) [Licenses](#license) are Fully Open Source > OSPS-LE-02.02

#### OSPS-LE-02.02

**Requirement:** While active, the [license](#license) for the [released software assets](#released-software-asset) MUST meet the OSI Open Source Definition or the FSF Free Software Definition.

**Recommendation:** If a different license is included with released software assets, ensure it is an approved license by the Open Source Initiative (OSI), or a free license as approved by the Free Software Foundation (FSF). Examples of such licenses include the MIT, BSD 2-clause, BSD 3-clause revised, Apache 2.0, Lesser GNU General Public License (LGPL), and the GNU General Public License (GPL). Note that the license for the released software assets may be different than the source code.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1
*   Maturity Level 2
*   Maturity Level 3

---

## OSPS-QA linter testing

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Quality > OSPS-QA-06 - Use Automated Testing in [CI/CD Pipelines](#ci-cd-pipeline) > OSPS-QA-06.02

#### OSPS-QA-06.02

**Requirement:** While active, [project](#project)'s documentation MUST clearly document when and how tests are run.

**Recommendation:** Add a section to the contributing documentation that explains how to run the tests locally and how to run the tests in the CI/CD pipeline. The documentation should explain what the tests are testing and how to interpret the results.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 3

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Quality > OSPS-QA-06 - Use Automated Testing in [CI/CD Pipelines](#ci-cd-pipeline)

### OSPS-QA-06 - Use Automated Testing in [CI/CD Pipelines](#ci-cd-pipeline)

Ensure that the project uses at least one automated test suite for the source code repository and clearly documents when and how tests are run.

---

## OSPS-SA SAST secret scanning dependency

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Build and Release > OSPS-BR-07 - Secure Secrets and Credentials > OSPS-BR-07.01

#### OSPS-BR-07.01

**Requirement:** The [project](#project) MUST prevent the unintentional storage of unencrypted [sensitive data](#sensitive-data), such as secrets and credentials, in the [version control system](#version-control-system).

**Recommendation:** Configure .gitignore or equivalent to exclude files that may contain sensitive information. Use pre-commit hooks and automated scanning tools to detect and prevent the inclusion of sensitive data in commits.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 1

--- [https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19] ---
### Build and Release > OSPS-BR-07 - Secure Secrets and Credentials > OSPS-BR-07.02

#### OSPS-BR-07.02

**Requirement:** The [project](#project) MUST define a policy for managing secrets and credentials used by the [project](#project). The policy should include guidelines for storing, accessing, and rotating secrets and credentials.

**Recommendation:** Document how secrets and credentials are managed and used within the project. This should include details on how secrets are stored (e.g., using a secrets management tool), how access is controlled, and how secrets are rotated or updated. Ensure that sensitive information is not hard-coded in the source code or stored in version control systems.

**Control applies to:**

*   Maturity Level 3

> AGENT

Context gathered. The baseline has three maturity levels that ratchet requirements upward, and I can see what Raven currently has vs what's missing. Before I design a remediation plan, one scoping question.

**Question 1 of ~3: Which baseline maturity level are you targeting?**

The OpenSSF Baseline 2026-02-19 is tiered — you pick the level that matches your project's maturity. Later levels include all earlier levels' requirements.

- **A) Level 1 only** — minimum bar. MFA, branch protection, basic docs (README, CONTRIBUTING, LICENSE), no secrets in repo, basic dep scanning. Mostly achievable with config + small additions; you already have most of this.
- **B) Level 2** — adds SECURITY.md with CVD policy, versioned releases with changelogs, signed release artifacts/hashes, SAST in CI, public build instructions, documented dep selection.
- **C) Level 3** — adds SBOM per release, threat model + attack-surface analysis, secret-management policy, documented test instructions, plus all L2/L1 controls. This is where it gets heavy.
- **D) Level 3 but pragmatic** — aim for L3, but defer the deepest items (formal threat model, SLSA provenance) to a follow-up milestone.

Which one fits? My recommendation based on what I see (dual license, pre-1.0, small maintainer set, edge + cloud deployments with an EE line): **B) Level 2** is the realistic sweet spot right now — it unlocks "visible security posture" without committing to SBOM + threat-model overhead, and L3 becomes a natural follow-up once you hit GA.

> DEVELOPER

go for level 2

> AGENT

Noted — targeting Level 2.

**Question 2 of ~3: Release model — do you cut tagged releases today?**

Several Level 2 controls key off "official releases" (versioned tags with changelogs + signed hashes). Looking at git log I see only dep bumps and feature PRs, no `v*` tags. What's the plan?

- **A) Yes, start cutting tagged releases now** — I'll design a release workflow (tagged `v0.x.x`, changelog, cosign-signed artifacts + Docker images) that the compliance controls bolt onto. This is the cleanest path.
- **B) Not yet — pre-GA, only running off `main` with edge/self-hosted Docker images** — L2 release controls (BR-02/04/06) effectively become "when we ship M-numbered milestone, produce a signed release." We can scaffold the workflow but it only fires on tag pushes.
- **C) Ship continuously; no formal releases** — doesn't really fit L2; we'd need to reframe every merge-to-main Docker image as a "release", which is more signing overhead.

Also one tightly related sub-question: the repo has both `LICENSE` and `ee-LICENSE`. **Is the public repo dual-licensed (core OSS + EE add-ons)?** That affects OSPS-LE-02 — the OSS-licensed portion must stay OSI-approved, and the EE portion either has to be clearly segregated or declared out-of-scope for the baseline.

My recommendation: **A + clarify EE scope**. Cutting real `v0.x.x` tags gives you the release artifact to sign/SBOM against; without it the L2 controls are hypothetical. And on the license side, I'd scope the baseline to the OSS portion only and document the boundary.

> DEVELOPER

go for recommended

> AGENT

Locked in: tagged releases + OSS-scope only (EE segregated).

**Question 3 of 3: Execution — one milestone, or fold into M2?**

Your memory shows M1 done, M2 next (issues #23–#32) with enriched specs. OSPS L2 compliance is ~8–12 discrete tasks. How do you want to track this?

- **A) New milestone "M-OSPS-L2"** with its own issues/PRs, run in parallel to M2 feature work. Clean separation, independent merge order, easier to present as a compliance artifact (e.g., for a future audit/badge).
- **B) Fold into M2** as additional issues. One milestone, one burndown. Riskier — security work competes with feature work for attention and may slip.
- **C) One bundled "compliance hardening" PR** covering all config/docs changes (SECURITY.md, release workflow, SAST, etc.). Fast, but giant PR is hard to review.

My recommendation: **A) new milestone**. Compliance is a coherent body of work with its own reviewers (CodeRabbit + you as sole CODEOWNER), and keeping it separate means M2 ships on time if compliance slips. Each control becomes one issue → one PR.

Once you answer this I'll propose 2–3 technical approaches for the actual remediation (tooling choices: cosign vs GPG signing, CodeQL vs Semgrep for SAST, etc.), then present the full design for approval.

## Three technical approaches

All three close the same L2 gaps. They differ in **tooling depth** and **how much future-proofing toward L3** you bake in now.

---

**Approach 1 — GitHub-native minimal**
- SAST: **CodeQL** (Go, Python, JS/TS — free, GH-hosted)
- Secret scanning: **GitHub Advanced Security** (public-repo native)
- Releases: manual GitHub Releases, tag-triggered workflow, `docker buildx` + manual SHA256 manifest
- Signing: **cosign keyless** (OIDC, no key management) on Docker images only
- Changelog: handwritten `CHANGELOG.md`
- SECURITY.md + governance docs authored manually

Pros: minimal new tooling, fast to implement. Cons: binary releases unsigned, no automation for changelogs, doesn't scale toward L3.

---

**Approach 2 — Sigstore + goreleaser (RECOMMENDED)**
- SAST: **CodeQL** (Go + Python + JS/TS)
- Secret scanning: **GitHub secret scanning** + **gitleaks** CI job as a second lens
- Releases: **goreleaser** for Go binaries (multi-arch), Docker image build/push, **cosign keyless** signing for both binaries + images, auto-generated checksums manifest (satisfies BR-06.01 cleanly)
- Changelog: **git-cliff** from your conventional commits (already used)
- CI hardening: pin actions to SHA (Dependabot handles updates), `permissions:` audit to satisfy AC-04.01
- SECURITY.md with 90-day CVD policy, `GitHub Security Advisories` for disclosure
- Docs: add "Dependency Policy" section to DEVELOPMENT.md (DO-06.01), ensure build instructions are discoverable (DO-07.01)

Pros: one tool (goreleaser) covers BR-02/04/06 for Go artifacts; cosign OIDC has no key rotation; git-cliff reuses existing commit discipline; CodeQL is zero-config. Natural path to L3 (syft plugs into goreleaser for SBOM later). Cons: goreleaser learning curve; Python worker + frontend aren't covered by goreleaser (need separate Docker-only release path).

---

**Approach 3 — Full SLSA L3 now**
- Everything in Approach 2, plus:
- **SLSA provenance** via `slsa-framework/slsa-github-generator` reusable workflows
- **Syft SBOMs** per release (CycloneDX + SPDX) — actually a L3 control, pre-delivered
- **Formal threat model doc** (STRIDE per service) — L3 control, pre-delivered
- Reusable workflow + all actions SHA-pinned

Pros: L3-ready immediately; strongest supply-chain story. Cons: 2–3× the work; threat model alone is days; SLSA generator requires workflow restructuring; over-builds for stated L2 goal.

---

**My recommendation: Approach 2.** It cleanly satisfies every L2 control, uses tools the OpenSSF itself endorses (Sigstore/cosign), and the SBOM + threat-model add-ons for L3 slot in without rework. Approach 1 leaves you re-doing the release pipeline next quarter; Approach 3 is scope creep vs. what you asked for.

Which approach do you want — A, B, or C?

## Section 1 / 4 — Scope & License Boundary

**In scope (OSPS L2 target):** the OSS portion of the repo under `LICENSE` — the API (`cmd/`, `internal/`), ai-worker (`ai-worker/`), frontend (`frontend/`), migrations, deploy manifests, and public docs. Releases that ship these artifacts (binaries, Docker images, frontend bundle) are the "released software assets" for OSPS-LE-02.02 and OSPS-BR-* controls.

**Out of scope:** the Enterprise Edition content under `ee-LICENSE` (currently `ee-LICENSE`, `ee-README.md`). These stay segregated. We'll add a short "Licensing" section to the root `README.md` making the boundary explicit, and add a statement in the OSS `LICENSE` area clarifying that EE files are covered by `ee-LICENSE` instead. EE artifacts are not released through the L2-compliant release pipeline; if/when they are, that's a separate compliance exercise.

**License verification action:** I'll confirm `LICENSE` is an OSI-approved license (likely already Apache 2.0 or MIT based on your stack) and that `ee-LICENSE` text clearly flags its non-OSS status. If the OSS license isn't OSI-approved, that's a fix-first blocker.

**Repository settings in scope (GitHub-enforced controls):**
- Branch protection on `main` (OSPS-AC-03.01, AC-03.02)
- MFA required for org + admins (OSPS-AC-01.01)
- Default collaborator permissions at minimum (OSPS-AC-02.01)
- Workflow `permissions:` keys audited (OSPS-AC-04.01)

These are config changes, not code — I'll capture them in the design doc as a checklist, not a PR.

## Section 2 / 4 — Control-by-Control Gap Matrix

All L1+L2 controls, current state, proposed action. Grouped by category; `✅`= already satisfied, `⚠`= partial, `❌` = gap.

### Access Control
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| AC-01.01 MFA on sensitive actions | ⚠ (user-level) | Enforce at org/repo level in GitHub settings |
| AC-02.01 New collaborators min-priv | ✅ | GitHub default — verify, no change |
| AC-03.01 Block direct commits to `main` | ⚠ | Enable branch protection: require PR + 1 review + status checks |
| AC-03.02 Prevent `main` deletion | ⚠ | Branch protection "restrict deletions" |
| AC-04.01 CI/CD least-privilege default | ⚠ | Audit all `.github/workflows/*.yml`, add top-level `permissions: {}` (empty) + explicit per-job grants |

### Build & Release
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| BR-01.01 Sanitize untrusted CI metadata | ⚠ | Audit workflows for unescaped `${{ github.event.* }}` interpolation |
| BR-01.03 Priv creds blocked on untrusted code | ⚠ | Grep for `pull_request_target`; remove or scope tightly |
| BR-02.01 Unique version identifier | ❌ | Adopt SemVer tags `v0.x.x`; enforce via release workflow |
| BR-03.01 Encrypted channels | ✅ | GitHub HTTPS — no change |
| BR-04.01 Release changelog | ❌ | `git-cliff` auto-generates from conventional commits → GitHub Release body |
| BR-05.01 Standardized dep tooling | ✅ | `go mod` / `npm` / `pip` all present |
| BR-06.01 Signed release + hashes | ❌ | **goreleaser + cosign keyless OIDC** → signed binaries, Docker images, checksums.txt, .sig, .pem |
| BR-07.01 No secrets in VCS | ⚠ | Add `gitleaks` CI job (fail PR); audit `.gitignore` |

### Documentation
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| DO-01.01 User guides | ✅ | `README.md` + `docs/` — verify completeness |
| DO-06.01 Dep selection/tracking docs | ❌ | Add "Dependency Policy" section to `DEVELOPMENT.md` |
| DO-07.01 Build instructions | ✅ | `DEVELOPMENT.md` exists |

### Governance
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| GV-01.01 List of members with sensitive access | ❌ | Create `MAINTAINERS.md` |
| GV-01.02 Roles/responsibilities | ❌ | Part of `MAINTAINERS.md` |
| GV-03.01 Contribution process | ✅ | `CONTRIBUTING.md` exists |
| GV-03.02 Contribution requirements | ⚠ | Extend `CONTRIBUTING.md`: coding standards, test reqs, submission rules |

### Legal
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| LE-01.01 Contributor legal assertion | ❌ | Add **DCO** (Developer Certificate of Origin) bot + sign-off requirement; or CLA Assistant |
| LE-02.01/02 OSI-approved source + release license | ⚠ | **Verify** `LICENSE` is OSI-approved (first action item); clarify EE boundary in README |

### Quality
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| QA-01.02 Public commit record | ✅ | GitHub native |
| QA-02.01 Dependency list | ✅ | `go.mod`, `package.json`, `requirements.txt` |
| QA-04.01 Codebase list (multi-repo) | N/A | Monorepo — no action |
| QA-05.01/02 No executables/binaries in VCS | ⚠ | Add `gitleaks` + file-size gate in pre-commit |

### Security Assessment
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| SA-01.01 Design/architecture docs | ❌ | Add `docs/architecture.md` with system diagram, actors, components (reuse project_architecture memory as seed) |
| SA-02.* SAST | ❌ | **CodeQL** workflow (Go, Python, JS/TS) |
| SA-03.* Dependency scanning | ✅ | govulncheck + Trivy + Dependabot already live |

### Vulnerability Management
| Control | State | Action |
|---|---|---|
| VM-01.01 CVD policy | ❌ | Create `SECURITY.md` — 90-day disclosure window, report channel (GH Security Advisories private reporting) |
| VM-02.01 Security contacts | ❌ | In `SECURITY.md` |
| VM-04.01 Publish discovered vulns | ❌ | Use **GitHub Security Advisories** — document the process in `SECURITY.md` |

## Section 3 / 4 — Release Pipeline Architecture

### Flow
```
dev commits → main (squash merge, conventional commits)
                  │
                  ▼
        git tag v0.x.x && push
                  │
                  ▼
       .github/workflows/release.yml  (tag trigger only)
                  │
     ┌────────────┼─────────────┬──────────────┬─────────────┐
     ▼            ▼             ▼              ▼             ▼
 preflight    changelog     go-binaries    docker-images   frontend-bundle
 (verify     (git-cliff     (goreleaser:   (buildx multi-  (npm run build
  tag on      since last     linux amd64/  arch amd64+     → dist/
  main,       tag → notes)   arm64, darwin arm64 → ghcr.   → frontend-
  SemVer)                    arm64;        io; cosign      vX.Y.Z.tgz;
                             SHA256SUMS;   sign each;      cosign sign)
                             cosign sign   build provenance
                             checksums)    attestation)
                                │             │                │
                                └──────┬──────┴────────────────┘
                                       ▼
                              publish-release
                           (GitHub Release: notes +
                            artifacts + .sig + .pem
                            + SHA256SUMS)
```

### Key choices

**Tag convention**: `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (SemVer, pre-1.0 means `v0.x.x`). Satisfies BR-02.01.

**Signing — cosign keyless (OIDC-based)**: no key management, no secret rotation. Each signature yields `artifact.sig` + `artifact.pem` (short-lived Fulcio cert, logged in Rekor transparency log). Satisfies BR-06.01. Verification published in `SECURITY.md`.

**Changelog — git-cliff**: reads conventional commits between last tag and current; produces markdown used for the Release body + appended to `CHANGELOG.md` on release branch bump. Satisfies BR-04.01.

**goreleaser handles**: Go binaries (API + any other `cmd/*` targets), SHA256SUMS generation, cosign invocation via `signs:` block, Docker buildx (optional — we'll keep Docker separate for clarity since the ai-worker is Python).

**Docker images**: `ghcr.io/<owner>/raven-api:v0.x.x` + `:latest`, `ghcr.io/<owner>/raven-ai-worker:v0.x.x` + `:latest`. Each cosign-signed. Multi-arch (amd64 + arm64) via buildx — required because of the Raspberry Pi/edge deployment constraint.

**Frontend bundle**: `frontend-vX.Y.Z.tgz` with `dist/` contents, cosign-signed blob, attached to GitHub Release. Users pulling the frontend separately (e.g., for CDN) can verify.

### Workflow permissions (per job, AC-04.01-compliant)

Top-level: `permissions: {}` (empty — no inherited grants).

Per-job:
- `preflight`, `changelog`: `contents: read`
- `go-binaries`: `contents: write`, `id-token: write` (OIDC for cosign)
- `docker-images`: `contents: read`, `packages: write`, `id-token: write`, `attestations: write`
- `frontend-bundle`: `contents: write`, `id-token: write`
- `publish-release`: `contents: write`

### Non-release CI additions (separate workflows)

- `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` — CodeQL analysis for Go, Python, JS/TS on PR + weekly schedule. **permissions**: `security-events: write`, `contents: read`, language-specific read. Satisfies SA-02.
- `.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml` — gitleaks-action on PR + push. Fails on any detected secret. Satisfies BR-07.01 + QA-05.
- Existing `security.yml` (Trivy + govulncheck) kept as-is — satisfies SA-03.

### Files to be created/modified

**New**:
- `.github/workflows/release.yml`
- `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`
- `.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml`
- `.goreleaser.yaml`
- `cliff.toml`
- `SECURITY.md`
- `MAINTAINERS.md`
- `docs/architecture.md`
- `docs/dependency-policy.md` (or section in `DEVELOPMENT.md`)
- `.github/workflows/dco.yml` (DCO check action) **or** migrate to CLA Assistant

**Modified**:
- `README.md` — add Licensing section clarifying OSS vs EE boundary, link to SECURITY.md + MAINTAINERS.md
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` — add coding standards / test reqs / sign-off section
- All existing `.github/workflows/*.yml` — add top-level `permissions: {}` + per-job grants

### Branch protection (GitHub UI, not code)

- Require PR before merge, 1 approval, CODEOWNERS review required
- Require status checks: `go`, `frontend`, `python`, `security`, `codeql`, `gitleaks`, `ci-required`
- Require signed commits (optional — DCO sign-off is weaker but less friction for contributors)
- Restrict deletion, disallow force pushes
- Require linear history (you're squash-only anyway)

## Section 4 / 4 — Milestone Breakdown & Sequencing

Milestone: **M-OSPS-L2**. Each row below = one issue = one PR. Phases are sequenced by dependency; within a phase, issues run in parallel.

### Phase 0 — Blocker verification (must pass first)

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-1 | License audit & fix | LE-02.01, LE-02.02 | If `LICENSE` isn't OSI-approved, stop and reset before anything else |

### Phase 1 — Documentation (parallel, after Phase 0)

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-2 | `SECURITY.md` + enable GH private vuln reporting | VM-01.01, VM-02.01, VM-04.01 | 90-day CVD policy, advisory workflow |
| OSPS-3 | `MAINTAINERS.md` | GV-01.01, GV-01.02 | Current maintainers + roles + access |
| OSPS-4 | Enhance `CONTRIBUTING.md` + add DCO sign-off requirement | GV-03.02, LE-01.01 | Coding standards, test reqs, `Signed-off-by` |
| OSPS-5 | `docs/architecture.md` | SA-01.01 | System diagram, actors, trust boundaries |
| OSPS-6 | Dependency policy (in `DEVELOPMENT.md` or `docs/dependency-policy.md`) | DO-06.01 | Selection criteria, Dependabot cadence, upstream trust |
| OSPS-7 | `README.md` licensing section + OSS/EE boundary | LE-02 scope | Links to SECURITY, MAINTAINERS, verification instructions |

### Phase 2 — Repo settings (owner-only, parallel to Phase 1)

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-8 | Branch protection + MFA + org settings checklist | AC-01.01, AC-02.01, AC-03.01, AC-03.02 | No code — GitHub UI checklist I'll document in the spec; verify via `gh api` |

### Phase 3 — CI hardening (parallel)

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-9 | Audit & lock `permissions:` in all workflows | AC-04.01, BR-01.01, BR-01.03 | Top-level `{}` + explicit per-job grants; grep for `pull_request_target` |
| OSPS-10 | `gitleaks` workflow + pre-commit hook | BR-07.01, QA-05.01/02 | PR-blocking |
| OSPS-11 | `CodeQL` workflow (Go, Python, JS/TS) | SA-02 | PR + weekly schedule |
| OSPS-12 | `DCO` check workflow | LE-01.01 | Blocks PRs without `Signed-off-by:` |

### Phase 4 — Release pipeline (depends on Phase 1 + 3)

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-13 | `.goreleaser.yaml` + Go binary release path | BR-02.01, BR-06.01 | cosign keyless signs checksums + artifacts |
| OSPS-14 | `cliff.toml` + changelog generation | BR-04.01 | Feeds release body |
| OSPS-15 | Release workflow — Docker images (API + ai-worker) | BR-06.01 | Multi-arch buildx, cosign sign, ghcr.io |
| OSPS-16 | Release workflow — frontend bundle | BR-06.01 | npm build → tarball → cosign sign |
| OSPS-17 | Dry-run `v0.1.0-rc1` tag + verify | All BR-* | Run verification commands; confirm all `.sig`/`.pem` artifacts exist and `cosign verify` succeeds |

### Phase 5 — Wrap-up

| # | Title | Controls | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSPS-18 | Self-assessment doc + baseline-version pin in README badge | — | `docs/compliance/osps-l2-2026-02-19.md` mapping each control to evidence (PR #, file, screenshot); badge links to assessment |

### Execution strategy

Per your milestone protocol (parallel worktree agents): Phase 1, 2, 3 can all run concurrently in separate worktrees. Phase 4 is sequential internally (release workflow PRs build on each other) but starts as soon as Phase 1 + 3 land. Each PR gets CodeRabbit + CI gates + squash-merge per project conventions.

**Total**: ~18 issues / PRs, estimated 2–4 weeks at steady cadence.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/osps-l2-spec/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-osps-l2-compliance-design.md",
  "content": "# OpenSSF Baseline L2 Compliance — Design\n\n**Date:** 2026-04-19\n**Baseline version:** [OSPS Baseline 2026-02-19](https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19)\n**Target maturity:** Level 2 (includes all Level 1 controls)\n**Milestone:** `M-OSPS-L2`\n**Owner:** @jobinlawrance\n\n## 1. Scope & Boundary\n\n### In scope (OSS portion)\n\nThe code, artifacts, and documentation released under `LICENSE`:\n\n- Backend API (`cmd/`, `internal/`, `pkg/`)\n- Python AI worker (`ai-worker/`)\n- Frontend (`frontend/`)\n- Database migrations (`migrations/`)\n- Deployment manifests (`deploy/`, `docker-compose*.yml`, `Dockerfile*`)\n- Public documentation (`docs/`, `README.md`, `DEVELOPMENT.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`)\n\nReleases of these artifacts (Go binaries, Docker images, frontend bundle) are the \"released software assets\" against which OSPS-LE-02.02 and OSPS-BR-* controls apply.\n\n### Out of scope (EE portion)\n\nFiles under `ee-LICENSE` (currently `ee-LICENSE`, `ee-README.md`, any future `ee-*` content). These are non-OSS and are not published through the L2-compliant release pipeline. The boundary is documented in the root `README.md`. If/when EE artifacts ship, that is a separate compliance exercise.\n\n### Prerequisites (fix-first blockers)\n\n- `LICENSE` MUST be verified as OSI-approved before anything else in this milestone proceeds. If it isn't, that is the first fix.\n- `ee-LICENSE` text MUST clearly mark its content as non-OSS (non-blocking for L2 scope, but recommended for clarity).\n\n## 2. Gap Matrix\n\nLegend: ✅ satisfied · ⚠ partial · ❌ missing\n\n### Access Control\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-01.01 MFA on sensitive actions | ⚠ | Enforce MFA at GitHub org/repo level |\n| AC-02.01 New collaborators min-priv | ✅ | GitHub default — verify, no change |\n| AC-03.01 Block direct commits to `main` | ⚠ | Branch protection: require PR + 1 review + status checks |\n| AC-03.02 Prevent `main` deletion | ⚠ | Branch protection \"restrict deletions\" |\n| AC-04.01 CI/CD least-privilege default | ⚠ | Top-level `permissions: {}` + explicit per-job grants in every workflow |\n\n### Build & Release\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| BR-01.01 Sanitize untrusted CI metadata | ⚠ | Audit workflows for unescaped `${{ github.event.* }}` |\n| BR-01.03 Priv creds blocked on untrusted code | ⚠ | Eliminate / tightly scope any `pull_request_target` |\n| BR-02.01 Unique version identifier | ❌ | Adopt SemVer tags `v0.x.x`; enforce in release workflow |\n| BR-03.01 Encrypted channels | ✅ | GitHub HTTPS — no change |\n| BR-04.01 Release changelog | ❌ | `git-cliff` auto-generates from conventional commits |\n| BR-05.01 Standardized dep tooling | ✅ | `go mod` / `npm` / `pip` already in use |\n| BR-06.01 Signed release + hashes | ❌ | `goreleaser` + `cosign` keyless (OIDC) → signed binaries, Docker images, `checksums.txt`, `.sig`, `.pem` |\n| BR-07.01 No secrets in VCS | ⚠ | Add `gitleaks` CI job (blocking) + audit `.gitignore` |\n\n### Documentation\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| DO-01.01 User guides | ✅ | `README.md` + `docs/` — verify completeness at release time |\n| DO-06.01 Dep selection/tracking docs | ❌ | Add \"Dependency Policy\" section to `DEVELOPMENT.md` or `docs/dependency-policy.md` |\n| DO-07.01 Build instructions | ✅ | `DEVELOPMENT.md` exists |\n\n### Governance\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| GV-01.01 List of members with sensitive access | ❌ | Create `MAINTAINERS.md` |\n| GV-01.02 Roles/responsibilities | ❌ | Part of `MAINTAINERS.md` |\n| GV-03.01 Contribution process | ✅ | `CONTRIBUTING.md` exists |\n| GV-03.02 Contribution requirements | ⚠ | Extend `CONTRIBUTING.md` with coding standards, test requirements, submission rules |\n\n### Legal\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| LE-01.01 Contributor legal assertion | ❌ | DCO (`Signed-off-by:`) via `dco` action; or CLA Assistant |\n| LE-02.01 OSI source license | ⚠ | Verify `LICENSE` is OSI-approved (prerequisite) |\n| LE-02.02 OSI released-asset license | ⚠ | Same verification; document in `README.md` licensing section |\n\n### Quality\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| QA-01.02 Public commit record | ✅ | GitHub native |\n| QA-02.01 Dependency list | ✅ | `go.mod`, `package.json`, `requirements.txt` |\n| QA-04.01 Codebase list (multi-repo) | N/A | Monorepo |\n| QA-05.01/02 No executables/binaries in VCS | ⚠ | `gitleaks` + file-size gate in pre-commit |\n\n### Security Assessment\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| SA-01.01 Design/architecture docs | ❌ | Add `docs/architecture.md` with system diagram, actors, trust boundaries |\n| SA-02 SAST | ❌ | CodeQL workflow (Go, Python, JS/TS), PR + weekly schedule |\n| SA-03 Dependency scanning | ✅ | `govulncheck` + Trivy + Dependabot already in `security.yml` |\n\n### Vulnerability Management\n\n| Control | State | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| VM-01.01 CVD policy | ❌ | `SECURITY.md` — 90-day disclosure window |\n| VM-02.01 Security contacts | ❌ | In `SECURITY.md` |\n| VM-04.01 Publish discovered vulns | ❌ | GitHub Security Advisories — process documented in `SECURITY.md` |\n\n**Totals:** 18 gaps, 9 partials. Most are docs + config; the release pipeline is the only substantial new build.\n\n## 3. Release Pipeline Architecture\n\n### Trigger\n\nTag push matching `v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+(-rc[0-9]+)?`. Tags are cut from `main` only; workflow preflight job rejects tags on other branches.\n\n### Flow\n\n```\ndev commits → main (squash-merged, conventional commits)\n                  │\n                  ▼\n        git tag v0.x.x && push\n                  │\n                  ▼\n       .github/workflows/release.yml  (tag trigger)\n                  │\n     ┌────────────┼─────────────┬──────────────┬─────────────┐\n     ▼            ▼             ▼              ▼             ▼\n preflight    changelog     go-binaries    docker-images   frontend-bundle\n (verify tag (git-cliff      (goreleaser:   (buildx multi-  (npm run build\n  on main,    since last     linux amd64/   arch amd64 +    → dist/\n  SemVer,     tag → release  arm64 + darwin arm64 → ghcr.   → frontend-\n  fail        notes.md)      arm64;         io; cosign      vX.Y.Z.tgz;\n  otherwise)                 SHA256SUMS;    sign each;      cosign sign)\n                             cosign sign    provenance\n                             checksums)     attestation)\n                                │             │                │\n                                └──────┬──────┴────────────────┘\n                                       ▼\n                              publish-release\n                           (GitHub Release: notes +\n                            artifacts + .sig + .pem\n                            + SHA256SUMS)\n```\n\n### Key choices\n\n- **Tag convention:** SemVer, `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (pre-1.0 → `v0.x.x`). Satisfies BR-02.01.\n- **Signing — cosign keyless (OIDC):** no key management; each signature produces `.sig` + `.pem` (short-lived Fulcio cert) and is recorded in the Rekor transparency log. Verification commands published in `SECURITY.md`. Satisfies BR-06.01.\n- **Changelog — git-cliff:** reads conventional commits since the last tag, emits release body + optional `CHANGELOG.md` append. Satisfies BR-04.01.\n- **goreleaser:** Go binaries (multi-arch), `SHA256SUMS` generation, cosign invocation via its `signs:` block.\n- **Docker images:** `ghcr.io/<owner>/raven-api:v0.x.x` + `:latest`, `ghcr.io/<owner>/raven-ai-worker:v0.x.x` + `:latest`. Each cosign-signed. Multi-arch (amd64 + arm64) via buildx — required for the Raspberry Pi/edge deployment target.\n- **Frontend bundle:** `frontend-vX.Y.Z.tgz` containing `dist/`; cosign-signed blob attached to the Release.\n\n### Workflow permissions (OSPS-AC-04.01)\n\nTop-level: `permissions: {}` (empty).\n\nPer-job:\n\n| Job | Permissions |\n|---|---|\n| `preflight` | `contents: read` |\n| `changelog` | `contents: read` |\n| `go-binaries` | `contents: write`, `id-token: write` |\n| `docker-images` | `contents: read`, `packages: write`, `id-token: write`, `attestations: write` |\n| `frontend-bundle` | `contents: write`, `id-token: write` |\n| `publish-release` | `contents: write` |\n\n### Non-release CI additions\n\n- **`.github/workflows/codeql.yml`** — CodeQL analysis for Go, Python, JS/TS. PR + weekly schedule. Satisfies SA-02.\n- **`.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml`** — gitleaks on PR + push; fails on any detected secret. Satisfies BR-07.01 + QA-05.\n- **`.github/workflows/dco.yml`** — DCO sign-off check. Satisfies LE-01.01.\n- **Existing `security.yml` (Trivy + govulncheck)** — retained as-is. Satisfies SA-03.\n\n### Files to create / modify\n\n**New:**\n\n- `.github/workflows/release.yml`\n- `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`\n- `.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml`\n- `.github/workflows/dco.yml`\n- `.goreleaser.yaml`\n- `cliff.toml`\n- `SECURITY.md`\n- `MAINTAINERS.md`\n- `docs/architecture.md`\n- `docs/dependency-policy.md` (or section appended to `DEVELOPMENT.md`)\n- `docs/compliance/osps-l2-2026-02-19.md` (self-assessment)\n\n**Modified:**\n\n- `README.md` — add \"Licensing\" section (OSS vs EE boundary) + links to `SECURITY.md`, `MAINTAINERS.md`, verification instructions\n- `CONTRIBUTING.md` — append coding standards, test requirements, `Signed-off-by:` sign-off section\n- All existing `.github/workflows/*.yml` — top-level `permissions: {}` + per-job grants\n\n### Branch protection (GitHub UI, no code)\n\n- Require PR before merge; 1 approving review\n- Require CODEOWNERS review\n- Required status checks: `go`, `frontend`, `python`, `security`, `codeql`, `gitleaks`, `dco`, `ci-required`\n- Disallow force pushes; restrict deletion\n- Linear history (already enforced by squash-only policy)\n\n## 4. Milestone Breakdown\n\nEach row below = one issue = one PR. Issues within a phase may run in parallel (separate worktrees per the milestone protocol).\n\n### Phase 0 — Blocker verification\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-1 | License audit & fix | LE-02.01, LE-02.02 |\n\nBlocks every other issue. If `LICENSE` is not OSI-approved, stop and resolve before proceeding.\n\n### Phase 1 — Documentation (parallel, after Phase 0)\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-2 | `SECURITY.md` + enable GH private vuln reporting | VM-01.01, VM-02.01, VM-04.01 |\n| OSPS-3 | `MAINTAINERS.md` | GV-01.01, GV-01.02 |\n| OSPS-4 | Extend `CONTRIBUTING.md` with standards + DCO sign-off | GV-03.02, LE-01.01 |\n| OSPS-5 | `docs/architecture.md` | SA-01.01 |\n| OSPS-6 | Dependency policy doc | DO-06.01 |\n| OSPS-7 | `README.md` licensing section + OSS/EE boundary | LE-02 scope |\n\n### Phase 2 — Repo settings (owner-only, parallel to Phase 1)\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-8 | Branch protection + MFA + org settings | AC-01.01, AC-02.01, AC-03.01, AC-03.02 |\n\nNo code. Checklist applied via GitHub UI / `gh api`. Evidence captured as a screenshot or `gh api` output in the PR description of OSPS-18.\n\n### Phase 3 — CI hardening (parallel)\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-9 | Audit & lock `permissions:` in all workflows | AC-04.01, BR-01.01, BR-01.03 |\n| OSPS-10 | `gitleaks` workflow + pre-commit hook | BR-07.01, QA-05.01, QA-05.02 |\n| OSPS-11 | CodeQL workflow (Go, Python, JS/TS) | SA-02 |\n| OSPS-12 | DCO check workflow | LE-01.01 |\n\n### Phase 4 — Release pipeline (depends on Phase 1 + 3)\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-13 | `.goreleaser.yaml` + Go binary release path | BR-02.01, BR-06.01 |\n| OSPS-14 | `cliff.toml` + changelog generation | BR-04.01 |\n| OSPS-15 | Release workflow — Docker images (API + ai-worker, multi-arch) | BR-06.01 |\n| OSPS-16 | Release workflow — frontend bundle | BR-06.01 |\n| OSPS-17 | Dry-run `v0.1.0-rc1` tag + verification | All BR-* |\n\nOSPS-17 is the integration gate: cuts a release candidate, runs all `cosign verify` commands end-to-end, confirms the Release page contains every expected artifact. Only after this passes can a production tag follow.\n\n### Phase 5 — Wrap-up\n\n| # | Title | Controls |\n|---|---|---|\n| OSPS-18 | Self-assessment doc + README badge | — |\n\n`docs/compliance/osps-l2-2026-02-19.md` maps every L1 + L2 control to its evidence (PR number, file path, screenshot). Badge in README links to the assessment. Baseline version is pinned in the filename so future upgrades are explicit.\n\n### Execution strategy\n\nPer the project's milestone protocol (parallel worktree agents, quality gates, CodeRabbit review, release on milestone completion):\n\n- Phases 1, 2, 3 run concurrently in separate worktrees after Phase 0 clears.\n- Phase 4 is internally sequential (each PR builds on the previous) but begins as soon as Phase 1 + 3 land.\n- Every PR: CodeRabbit review, CI gates (including the new `codeql`, `gitleaks`, `dco` checks once merged), squash-merge, no AI-attribution trailers.\n- Milestone closes with OSPS-17 dry-run passing and OSPS-18 assessment merged.\n\n**Estimated duration:** 2–4 weeks at steady cadence, parallelism-dependent.\n\n## 5. Verification\n\nEach control is verified one of three ways:\n\n- **Artifact in repo** (e.g., `SECURITY.md` exists and satisfies the required fields).\n- **CI job passes** (e.g., `codeql` green on every PR).\n- **External check** — documented command in OSPS-18 self-assessment. Example for BR-06.01:\n\n  ```bash\n  cosign verify-blob \\\n    --certificate checksums.txt.pem \\\n    --signature checksums.txt.sig \\\n    --certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>' \\\n    --certificate-oidc-issuer 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com' \\\n    checksums.txt\n  ```\n\nEach of these lands in `docs/compliance/osps-l2-2026-02-19.md` as the final deliverable.\n\n## 6. Non-goals\n\n- Level 3 controls (SBOM per release, formal threat model + attack-surface analysis, full secret-management policy) — tracked as a future milestone. Tooling choices here (cosign, goreleaser, CodeQL) are compatible with a future L3 push; `syft` can plug into goreleaser for SBOM and a STRIDE-based threat model can be appended to `docs/architecture.md`.\n- EE compliance — out of scope; tracked separately if/when EE artifacts are publicly released.\n- SLSA L3 provenance generator (`slsa-framework/slsa-github-generator`) — compatible with this design but not required for L2; deferred.\n"
}

## Spec Review

**Status:** Approved

The spec is complete, internally consistent, and implementation-ready. Key strengths:

- **Scope is crisp**: OSS-only boundary declared upfront (section 1); EE explicitly out of scope; fix-first prerequisite (LICENSE OSI check) identified as a Phase 0 blocker.
- **Gap matrix is exhaustive**: Every OSPS L1+L2 control area (AC, BR, DO, GV, LE, QA, SA, VM) has rows with explicit state markers (satisfied / partial / missing / N/A). QA-04.01 is correctly marked N/A with rationale (monorepo).
- **Architecture is concrete**: Release flow diagram names every job, every artifact extension (`.sig`/`.pem`/SHA256SUMS), every permission grant per job, and every trigger condition. Tool choices (goreleaser, cosign keyless via OIDC, git-cliff, buildx multi-arch for Pi) trace back to specific controls.
- **Milestone breakdown is actionable**: 18 issues bucketed into 6 phases with explicit parallelism rules; dependencies stated (Phase 4 depends on 1+3); OSPS-17 is called out as the integration gate before production release.
- **Verification path is concrete**: Three verification modes defined; cosign verify command is spelled out; self-assessment doc path pinned to baseline version (`osps-l2-2026-02-19.md`) so future baseline bumps are explicit.
- **YAGNI discipline is good**: Non-goals section (section 6) explicitly defers L3 (SBOM, threat model, SLSA L3) and EE compliance; notes forward-compatibility without scope creep.

**Recommendations (advisory):**
- Section 2's totals ("18 gaps, 9 partials") does not count rows in the matrix as drawn (~13 ❌ + ~11 ⚠ by my count). Not a planning blocker, but worth reconciling when the assessment doc lands.
- OSPS-7 (README licensing) arguably overlaps with OSPS-1 (license audit) — consider whether they should merge, or clarify that OSPS-1 is the OSI verification and OSPS-7 is the README narrative. Current phrasing is workable.
- The cosign verify example in section 5 uses `--certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>'` — OSPS-18 should replace the placeholder with the actual owner/repo before publication; worth calling out as a concrete sub-task there.

Spec path: `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/osps-l2-spec/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-osps-l2-compliance-design.md`

Extend CONTRIBUTING.md (GV-03.02)\n\nAdd/strengthen the following sections if missing or thin:\n\n- **Coding standards** — link to `.golangci.yml`, ruff config, eslint config; state that all linters must pass before push (you can see this rule in the feedback memory too)\n- **Testing requirements** — unit tests required for new features; integration tests for cross-service; Playwright for new frontend flows; all green locally before push\n- **Commit style** — conventional commits (`feat:`, `fix:`, `docs:`, `chore:`, `ci:`, `refactor:`, `deps:`); squash-merge only; no force-push to main\n- **DCO sign-off required** — every commit must include `Signed-off-by: Name <email>`; set up via `git commit -s`; link to https://developercertificate.org for the DCO text\n- **Submission guidelines** — PR template auto-populates; fill out all sections; link an issue where applicable; CI + CodeRabbit must pass; at least 1 maintainer review\n\nPreserve any existing content; only add or strengthen. Do not remove valid content.\n\n### 2. Create .github/workflows/dco.yml (LE-01.01)\n\nUse the `dco-check-action` or equivalent action that verifies every commit in a PR has a `Signed-off-by:` line. Recommended: `tim-actions/dco@2370c00d1db87af1b77bcf6cad3d0c1f28f37cf7` (or latest pinned SHA from https://github.com/tim-actions/dco).\n\nMinimal workflow:\n```yaml\nname: DCO\non:\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n  pull-requests: read\njobs:\n  dco-check:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@<pinned SHA>  # pin the SHA, look up the actions/checkout@v6 digest\n        with:\n          fetch-depth: 0\n      - uses: tim-actions/dco@<pinned SHA>\n```\n\nImportant: PIN the action to a commit SHA (not a tag) since we're establishing signing discipline. You can find the pinned SHA by running `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/refs/tags/<version>`. For actions/checkout@v6 and other actions/* we already use in other workflows in `.github/workflows/`, reuse the same SHA pinning style those use, or bring them all to SHA pinning if they aren't yet.\n\n### 3. Don't invent components.\n\n### 2. docs/dependency-policy.md (DO-06.01 — describe how project selects, obtains, tracks dependencies)\n\nSections:\n- **Selection criteria**: preference for OSI-licensed packages with active maintenance, recent releases, >1 maintainer, security track record\n- **Obtaining**: Go via `go mod`, Python via `pip` with `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml` in ai-worker, JS via `npm`/`bun` in frontend, Docker base images from official org repos (postgres, redis/valkey, etc.), GitHub Actions pinned by SHA\n- **Tracking**:\n  - Dependabot configured for gomod, pip, npm, docker, github-actions (weekly) — link to `.github/dependabot.yml`\n  - Trivy filesystem scan weekly + on push/PR — link to `.github/workflows/security.yml`\n  - govulncheck on every push/PR\n  - CodeQL (once OSPS-11 lands)\n- **Review & update cadence**: PRs from Dependabot reviewed within 7 days; CVEs with CVSS ≥7 triaged within 72h\n- **Vendoring / pinning policy**: dependencies not vendored; lockfiles committed (go.sum, bun.lock, requirements lock)\n- **Excluded / quarantined dependencies**: link to `.trivyignore` and explain when entries get added\n- **Relationship to license compliance**: note that direct deps must be compatible with Apache 2.0 for the OSS artifact; copyleft (GPL/AGPL) requires maintainer review\n\n### 3. Modify README.md — add \"Licensing\" section\n\nLocate the README. Add (or insert near the top, after the tagline) a \"Licensing\" section that says:\n\n```markdown\n## Licensing\n\nRaven is dual-licensed:\n\n- **Open-source portion** — everything in this repository is licensed under the [Apache License 2.0](./LICENSE) **except** for files and directories explicitly marked with an `ee-` prefix.\n- **Enterprise portion** — files prefixed with `ee-` (e.g., `ee-LICENSE`, `ee-README.md`) are covered by the [Raven Enterprise License](./ee-LICENSE) and are not open-source.\n\nSecurity issues are coordinated via [SECURITY.md](./SECURITY.md). Maintainers are listed in [MAINTAINERS.md](./MAINTAINERS.md).\n\nThe open-source portion targets [OpenSSF Baseline](https://baseline.openssf.org/versions/2026-02-19) Level 2 compliance.\n```\n\nIf the README already has a licensing section, enhance it rather than duplicate. Keep all other README content intact.\n\n## Validation\n- `markdownlint-cli2 docs/architecture.md docs/dependency-policy.md README.md` if installed\n- Render mermaid blocks mentally / via `mmdc` if installed; otherwise verify the syntax looks right\n- Verify links resolve (relative paths)\n\n## Commit, push, PR, auto-merge\n```bash\ngit add docs/architecture.md docs/dependency-policy.md README.md\ngit commit -s -m \"docs(osps): add architecture + dependency policy; README licensing section\n\nImplements OSPS-SA-01.01 (design descriptions), OSPS-DO-06.01\n(dependency selection / tracking policy), and clarifies the\nOSS vs EE licensing boundary in README for OSPS-LE-02 scope.\"\ngit push -u origin docs/osps-arch-deps-readme\ngh pr create --title \"docs(osps): architecture + dependency policy + README licensing\" --body \"...\"\ngh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash\n```\n\n## Critical rules\n- NO AI attribution\n- NO --no-verify\n- Commit with -s (Signed-off-by) since DCO will be enforced\n- Describe the system as it actually is — don't fabricate components you can't verify\n\nReturn: PR number + URL + any uncertainty about architecture details. push on main (paths: `**/*.go`, `**/*.py`, `**/*.ts`, `**/*.tsx`, `**/*.js`, `**/*.vue`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `frontend/package.json`, `frontend/bun.lock`, `ai-worker/pyproject.toml`, `ai-worker/requirements*.txt`, `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`)\n  - pull_request on main (same paths)\n  - schedule: weekly, e.g., `cron: '23 6 * * 1'` (offset from security.yml's Monday 06:00 to avoid load spike)\n- Top-level `permissions: {}` (empty)\n- Matrix job `analyze` with language: [go, python, javascript-typescript]\n- Per-job permissions:\n  - `security-events: write`\n  - `contents: read`\n  - `actions: read`\n  - `packages: read` (only needed for private deps; keep minimal)\n- Use `github/codeql-action/init@<pinned SHA>`, `github/codeql-action/autobuild@<pinned SHA>`, `github/codeql-action/analyze@<pinned SHA>`\n- Pin action SHAs (find current via `gh api repos/github/codeql-action/commits/<tag>` or use the latest stable tag and resolve SHA)\n- For Go: use autobuild (it handles `go build ./...`)\n- For JavaScript/TypeScript: specify `build-mode: none` (no build required for JS/TS)\n- For Python: `build-mode: none`\n- Include a query suite: `queries: security-and-quality` (stricter than default `security-extended`) OR `security-extended` as a balance — pick `security-extended` for L2, we're not trying to drown in quality nits\n- Use `fail-on: error` to fail the job on analysis errors, not findings (findings show up as security alerts)\n\nExample skeleton (you fill in pinned SHAs and verify against latest docs):\n\n```yaml\nname: CodeQL\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n    paths:\n      - 'ai-worker/pyproject.toml'\n      - 'ai-worker/requirements*.txt'\n      - '.github/workflows/codeql.yml'\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n    paths:\n      - '**/*.go'\n      - '**/*.py'\n      - '**/*.ts'\n      - '**/*.tsx'\n      - '**/*.js'\n      - '**/*.vue'\n      - 'go.mod'\n      - 'go.sum'\n      - 'frontend/package.json'\n      - 'frontend/bun.lock'\n      - 'ai-worker/pyproject.toml'\n      - language: go\n            build-mode: autobuild\n          - language: python\n            build-mode: none\n          - language: javascript-typescript\n            build-mode: none\n    steps:\n      - name: Initialize CodeQL\n        uses: github/codeql-action/init@<SHA>\n        with:\n          languages: ${{ matrix.language }}\n          build-mode: ${{ matrix.build-mode }}\n          queries: security-extended\n\n      - name: Autobuild\n        if: matrix.build-mode == 'autobuild'\n        uses: github/codeql-action/autobuild@<SHA>\n\n      - name: Perform CodeQL Analysis\n        uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@<SHA>\n        with:\n          category: \"/language:${{ matrix.language }}\"\n```\n\n## Validation\n- `yamllint .github/workflows/codeql.yml`\n- `actionlint .github/workflows/codeql.yml` (if available, install via `go install github.com/rhysd/actionlint/cmd/actionlint@latest`)\n- If you have `gh` extensions like `gh workflow view`, check after push\n\n## Commit, push, PR, auto-merge\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/codeql.yml\ngit commit -s -m \"ci(osps): add CodeQL SAST workflow for Go, Python, JS/TS\n\nImplements OSPS-SA-02 (static application security testing)\nfrom the OpenSSF Baseline L2 target. Uses security-extended query suite.\"\ngit push -u origin ci/osps-codeql\ngh pr create --title \"ci(osps): add CodeQL SAST workflow\" --body \"...\"\ngh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash\n```\n\n## Critical rules\n- NO AI attribution in commit or PR body\n- NO --no-verify\n- Pin ALL action SHAs (no `@v6` float) — use `gh api` to resolve SHA\n- Commit with `git commit -s`\n\nReturn: PR number + URL + the resolved SHAs you pinned. Add allowlist for those paths.\n\n## Worktree setup\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven\ngit fetch origin main\ngit worktree add .worktrees/osps-gitleaks -b ci/osps-gitleaks origin/main\ncd .worktrees/osps-gitleaks\nls -la | grep env  # see env files\ncat .env.example | head -20  # confirm it's placeholders\n```\n\n## Files to create\n\n### 1. .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml\n\nUse the official `gitleaks/gitleaks-action` pinned to a SHA. Workflow design:\n\n- Name: \"Secret Scanning\"\n- Triggers:\n  - push on main (all paths; secrets can be added anywhere)\n  - pull_request on main (all paths, all changed files)\n  - schedule: weekly on Monday at a different UTC offset from security.yml/codeql.yml to avoid load spikes, e.g., `cron: '46 6 * * 1'`\n- Top-level `permissions: {}` (empty)\n- Job `scan`:\n  - `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`\n  - `permissions: { contents: read, pull-requests: read }`\n  - Steps:\n    1. `actions/checkout@<SHA>` with `fetch-depth: 0` (needed for commit history scan)\n    2. `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@<SHA>` with env `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GITLEAKS_LICENSE` (comment note that the latter is only needed for orgs; remove or keep as `${{ secrets.GITLEAKS_LICENSE }}` unset for public repos)\n  - `exit-code: 1` behavior: fail PR on detection\n\nPin action SHAs. Resolve via `gh api repos/gitleaks/gitleaks-action/commits/<tag>` and `gh api repos/actions/checkout/commits/<tag>`.\n\n### 2. .gitleaks.toml (configuration)\n\nCreate `.gitleaks.toml` at repo root that:\n- Extends the default ruleset: `[extend] useDefault = true`\n- Adds an allowlist block for known-safe paths:\n  ```toml\n  [allowlist]\n  description = \"Global allowlist\"\n  paths = [\n    '''(?i)\\.env\\.example$''',\n    '''(?i)\\.env\\.ci$''',\n    '''(?i)testdata/''',\n    '''(?i)fixtures/''',\n    '''(?i)node_modules/''',\n    '''(?i)vendor/''',\n    '''(?i)backup/''',\n    '''go\\.sum$''',\n    '''bun\\.lock$''',\n    '''\\.worktrees/''',\n  ]\n  ```\n- Includes a note at top of file explaining why each path is allowlisted (env.example = placeholders, testdata = fixtures, etc.)\n\nBefore finalizing the allowlist, scan the repo quickly to see what WOULD trip:\n```bash\n# Install gitleaks locally if not present (optional)\n# brew install gitleaks  OR\n# docker run --rm -v $(pwd):/path zricethezav/gitleaks:latest detect --source /path --no-git -v\n```\nIf any real-looking strings come up in ordinary source files (not env.example), STOP and ask before adding broad allowlists. The goal is to catch real secrets, not to suppress everything.\n\n### 3. (Optional) docs/security/secret-scanning.md\n\nA short doc explaining:\n- How the workflow runs\n- What to do if gitleaks flags your PR (first: is it a real secret? If yes, rotate NOW; if no, add narrow allowlist rule)\n- How to run gitleaks locally before pushing: `gitleaks detect --source . --no-git -v`\n\nSkip this file if scope is getting too big; it's a nice-to-have.\n\n## Validation\n- `yamllint .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml`\n- `actionlint .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml` if available\n- If gitleaks is installed locally: `gitleaks detect --source . --config .gitleaks.toml --no-git -v` — should report 0 findings (or at most a handful in allowlisted paths) BEFORE you commit. If real secrets found in the repo history, stop and report.\n\n## Commit, push, PR, auto-merge\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml .gitleaks.toml\n# (optionally: docs/security/secret-scanning.md)\ngit commit -s -m \"ci(osps): add gitleaks secret-scanning workflow\n\nImplements OSPS-BR-07.01 (prevent unencrypted secrets in VCS)\nand OSPS-QA-05.01/02 (no generated or unreviewable binary\nartifacts in VCS) from the OpenSSF Baseline L2 target.\n\nFails PRs on any detected secret. Allowlist covers known-safe\npaths (.env.example, .env.ci, testdata, fixtures, vendor).\"\ngit push -u origin ci/osps-gitleaks\ngh pr create --title \"ci(osps): add gitleaks secret-scanning workflow\" --body \"...\"\ngh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash\n```\n\n## Critical rules\n- NO AI attribution\n- NO --no-verify\n- Pin action SHAs\n- Commit with -s (DCO sign-off)\n- If you find real secrets during the pre-scan, STOP and report instead of committing\n\nReturn: PR number + URL + pre-scan result summary + any real secrets found (or \"none\"). {

  "subagent_type": "general-purpose",
  "prompt": "Create a PR that audits and locks down `permissions:` keys on all existing GitHub Actions workflows. Implements OSPS-9 (AC-04.01 + BR-01.01 + BR-01.03) from the OpenSSF Baseline L2 spec at /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/osps-l2-spec/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-osps-l2-compliance-design.md.\n\n## Scope\nModify EXISTING workflows only:\n- .github/workflows/go.yml\n- .github/workflows/python.yml\n- .github/workflows/frontend.yml\n- .github/workflows/docker.yml\n- .github/workflows/security.yml\n- .github/workflows/ci-required.yml\n- .github/workflows/pages.yml\n- .github/workflows/landing.yml\n- .github/workflows/claude.yml\n- .github/workflows/opencode.yml\n\nDo NOT touch:\n- .github/workflows/codeql.yml (being added in parallel PR ci/osps-codeql)\n- .github/workflows/gitleaks.yml (being added in parallel PR ci/osps-gitleaks)\n- .github/workflows/dco.yml (being added in parallel PR docs/osps-contributing-dco)\n\n## Project facts\n- Repo: github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven (public)\n- Workflows run on ubuntu-latest, use actions like actions/checkout, actions/setup-go, actions/setup-python, actions/setup-node, aquasecurity/trivy-action\n- Some workflows deploy (pages.yml, landing.yml likely use `pages: write`); some build Docker and push to registry (docker.yml likely uses `packages: write`); most are read-only test/lint.\n\n## Worktree setup\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven\ngit fetch origin main\ngit worktree add .worktrees/osps-permissions -b ci/osps-workflow-permissions origin/main\ncd .worktrees/osps-permissions\nls -la .github/workflows/\n```\n\n## What to do, per workflow\n\nFor EACH existing workflow file, do this audit:\n\n1. **Read the workflow** and figure out what it actually does per job.\n2. **Add top-level `permissions: {}` (empty)** right below `on:` if not already present.\n3. **Add explicit `permissions:` block on each job** with the minimum needed. Typical patterns:\n   - Pure test/lint job → `contents: read`\n   - Job uploading SARIF to code-scanning → `security-events: write` + `contents: read`\n   - Job pushing to GHCR / Docker Hub → `packages: write` + `contents: read`\n   - Job deploying to GitHub Pages → `pages: write` + `id-token: write` + `contents: read`\n   - Job using cosign OIDC → `id-token: write` + relevant others\n   - Job commenting on PRs → `pull-requests: write`\n4. **Audit for `pull_request_target`** (OSPS-BR-01.03). Grep all workflow files for `pull_request_target`. If found:\n   - Verify the checked-out ref is NOT the PR head in a way that would expose secrets to untrusted code.\n   - Either remove `pull_request_target` in favor of `pull_request` (safer), OR tightly scope by filtering `github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository` to allow first-party PRs only.\n   - If you find a genuinely untrusted `pull_request_target` with secrets exposed, this is a security issue — STOP and report rather than silently changing.\n5. **Audit for unsafe interpolation** (OSPS-BR-01.01). Grep for `${{ github.event.* }}` inside `run:` blocks or shell commands. Any that come from user-controlled fields (issue titles, PR bodies, branch names, commit messages) are injection vectors. Fix by passing through env vars:\n   ```yaml\n   env:\n     TITLE: ${{ github.event.pull_request.title }}\n   run: |\n     echo \"$TITLE\"\n   ```\n   If none found, state so in the PR body.\n\n## Validation per workflow\nAfter each edit, verify YAML parses:\n```bash\npython -c \"import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/<name>.yml'))\"\n```\nRun `actionlint` across all workflows if installed:\n```bash\nactionlint .github/workflows/*.yml\n```\n\n## Compile a summary\n\nIn the PR body, include a table:\n\n| Workflow | Top-level perms | Jobs locked | pull_request_target? | Unsafe interpolation found |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| go.yml | `{}` | 3/3 | no | no |\n| ... | ... | ... | ... | ... |\n\n## Commit, push, PR, auto-merge\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/\ngit commit -s -m \"ci(osps): lock down workflow permissions to least privilege\n\nAdds top-level empty permissions + explicit per-job grants to\nall existing workflows. Implements OSPS-AC-04.01 (CI/CD least\nprivilege), OSPS-BR-01.01 (sanitize untrusted CI metadata),\nand OSPS-BR-01.03 (prevent privileged credentials on untrusted\ncode) from the OpenSSF Baseline L2 target.\n\nAudited for pull_request_target and unsafe \\${{ github.event.* }}\ninterpolation patterns.\"\ngit push -u origin ci/osps-workflow-permissions\ngh pr create --title \"ci(osps): lock down workflow permissions to least privilege\" --body \"...\"\ngh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash\n```\n\n## Critical rules\n- NO AI attribution\n- NO --no-verify\n- DON'T broaden permissions in the name of \"consistency\" — tighten only. If unclear what a job needs, err on `contents: read` and let CI fail once so we can observe what's actually needed.\n- If you find a real security issue (exposed secrets in pull_request_target, unsanitized interpolation in production), STOP and escalate in the return message.\n- Commit with `git commit -s`\n\nReturn: PR number + URL + the audit table summary + any security issues discovered. Keep under 400 words.",
  "run_in_background": true
}

1	# Contributing to Raven
2	
3	## Before You Start
4	
5	Open an issue first for anything beyond a trivial fix. This prevents duplicate effort and lets us align on approach before you invest time writing code.
6	
7	## Branch Naming
8	
9	| Prefix | When |
10	|--------|------|
11	| `feat/` | New feature |
12	| `fix/` | Bug fix |
13	| `refactor/` | Code restructure, no behaviour change |
14	| `ci/` | CI/CD changes |
15	| `chore/` | Tooling, deps, config |
16	| `deps/` | Dependency bumps |
17	
18	Example: `feat/semantic-cache`, `fix/voice-session-timeout`
19	
20	## Commit Style
21	
22	- Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) — `feat:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, etc.
23	- Keep the subject line under 72 characters
24	- No AI attribution trailers (`Co-Authored-By:` etc.)
25	
26	## Pull Request Workflow
27	
28	1. Branch off `main`
29	2. Make your changes with passing tests and lint
30	3. Open a PR against `main`
31	4. Immediately queue auto-merge after creation:
32	
33	```bash
34	gh pr create --title "..." --body "..." --base main
35	gh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash
36	```
37	
38	PRs squash-merge only — no regular merge, no rebase-merge.
39	
40	## Quality Gates
41	
42	All of these must pass **locally** before pushing. CI will reject if they don't.
43	
44	### Go (backend)
45	
46	```bash
47	# Build
48	go build ./...
49	
50	# Tests (including integration tests — needs Docker)
51	go test -race -timeout 30m ./...
52	
53	# Lint
54	golangci-lint run
55	```
56	
57	### Python (AI worker)
58	
59	```bash
60	cd ai-worker
61	ruff check .
62	ruff format --check .
63	pytest
64	```
65	
66	### Frontend
67	
68	```bash
69	cd frontend
70	npm run lint          # ESLint
71	npx tsc --noEmit      # TypeScript check
72	npm run test:unit     # Vitest unit tests
73	npm run build         # Production build (catches type errors vite misses)
74	```
75	
76	### End-to-End (Playwright)
77	
78	E2E tests run against a live stack. See [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md) for setup.
79	
80	```bash
81	cd frontend
82	npm run test:e2e
83	```
84	
85	## Code Style
86	
87	### Go
88	
89	- Follow standard Go conventions (`gofmt`, `golangci-lint`)
90	- Handler → Service → Repository layering — no direct DB access from handlers
91	- Return `*apierror.AppError` at service boundaries; let `apierror.ErrorHandler()` middleware render the response
92	- Use `pgx.Tx` with `db.WithOrgID` for all tenant-scoped DB operations (RLS enforcement)
93	- Wrap errors: `fmt.Errorf("ServiceName.MethodName: %w", err)`
94	
95	### Python
96	
97	- `ruff` for formatting and linting (configured in `pyproject.toml`)
98	- Type hints on all public functions
99	- `structlog` for structured logging — no bare `print()`
100	
101	### Vue / TypeScript
102	
103	- Composition API with `<script setup>` — no Options API
104	- Pinia for all shared state
105	- Tailwind CSS only — no additional CSS frameworks
106	- `useAuthStore()` for auth; `useFeatureFlag()` for PostHog feature flags
107	
108	## Testing Expectations
109	
110	- **Unit tests**: every new function with logic gets a test
111	- **Integration tests** (Go): use `testutil.NewTestDB()` — it spins up a real Postgres via testcontainers
112	- **No mocking the database** in integration tests — we got burned by mock/prod divergence before
113	- **Playwright E2E**: cover the happy path for any new user-facing flow
114	
115	## Database Migrations
116	
117	Migrations live in `migrations/` and use [goose](https://github.com/pressly/goose).
118	
119	```bash
120	# Apply all pending migrations
121	make migrate-up
122	
123	# Roll back one migration
124	make migrate-down
125	```
126	
127	Rules:
128	- Never edit an existing migration file after it has been merged to `main`
129	- Every new table needs RLS policies — see existing migrations for the pattern
130	- Column renames = new migration with `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN`
131	
132	## Never
133	
134	- Push directly to `main`
135	- Use `--no-verify` to skip hooks
136	- Amend published commits
137	- Force-push to `main`
138	
```
